6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8 
ECB to hike once more: Euro area activity has proved more 
resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 growth estimate 
has been revised up to 0.9%, from 0.5% as recently as July, 
with ECB staff projections upgraded to  1.4% for 2027 and 
1.5% for 2028. Manufacturing PMIs have improved to 52.7 
regionally and services PMI to 53.0, helped by German fiscal 
delivery and falling household savings rates. A softer Q3 looks 
weather-driven, due to heatwaves and low Rhine water levels, 
though higher energy prices and real yields still cap demand. 
Headline inflation hit a near three -year high of 3.3% y/y in 
August and may peak around 3.4 -3.6% in Q4 2026 before 
easing through 2027. Underlying pressures look calmer: core 
inflation drifted down to 2.4%, services eased, and negotiated 
wage growth slowed to 2.4% y/y in Q2, down from a peak of 
5.6% in 2024 . Long -term inflation expectations remain 
anchored with no evidence of second -round effects. Inflation 
risks are two -sided – oil above USD 100/bbl and gas above 
EUR 70/MWh are fuelling near -term inflation, which in turn 
should hurt demand, driving inflation below 2% by H2 2027. 
The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.50% in September, the 
top of neutral estimates. We see scope for one more rate hike 
to 2.75% by year -end before a pause in H1 2027. As such 
pricing of 95bps more tightening toward an above-3% terminal 
rate appears too hawkish. We expect easing of energy-driven 
price pressures to allow for ECB rate cuts by Q3 2027. 
China to boost fiscal spending to support growth. China 
is entering a stabilisation phase in which production is firming, 
but the recovery remains narrow. The official manufacturing 
PMI rose to 49.8 in August from 49.2, with new orders, export 
orders and production back in expansionary territory, while 
export growth accelerated to 25.0% y/y. Momentum, however, 
is concentrated in high -tech and export manufacturing: retail 
sales, fixed-asset investment and credit growth remain soft, 
and the property downturn has yet to bottom. Full -year 2026 
growth should land near 4.5 –4.6%, broadly in line with the 
official 4.5–5% target, as domestic demand stays weak. 
Reflation is under way, but shallow and cost-driven rather than 
demand-led. Headline consumer inflation edged up to 0.8% 
y/y in August and producer inflation to 3.8%, lifted by higher 
commodity and energy prices and by official efforts to curb 
excessive price-cutting. Full-year inflation is expected around 
0.8–1.0%, against roughly zero in 2025, with producer 
inflation swinging from −2.6% to about +2.5%. The persistent 
supply-demand imbalance should cap the reflation impulse. 
Policy settings remain supportive, but with no rush to 
stimulate. Fiscal policy takes the primary role via faster 
spending and utilisation of bond proceeds, with broad fiscal 
spending potentially accelerating in H2 versus a sharp 
contraction in H1, and the augmented fiscal deficit widening 
modestly. Monetary policy plays a complementary role: a 
25bps cut in bank reserve requirements is likely, while the 
policy rate should stay unchanged. Slow public financing 
raises the likelihood of further policy fine-tuning this autumn. 
BoJ to hike once a quarter until Q2 2027. Japan's economy 
is proving more resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 
growth estimate has been revised up over the past month to 
0.8% from 0.6%. Exports rose 19% y/y in August, a twelfth 
consecutive y/y growth, while core machinery and machine 
tool orders indicate higher borrowing costs have yet to dent 
corporate investment appetite. Real wages climbed 2.4% y/y 
in July, the strongest since May 2021. The weak link is the 
household sector: spending continued to contract y/y, leaving 
an unusual two -speed economy in which external and 
corporate demand carry growth while domestic demand lags. 
Underlying inflation sits close to the 2% target, with pressure 
building upstream rather than from demand. Energy and 
commodity prices have risen sharply and earlier yen 
weakness has amplified import costs, with producer inflation 
running well ahead of CPI . The 10-year breakeven inflation 
has risen close to May’s record high of 2.3% amid growing risk 
of pass-through from producer to consumer prices.  
Bank of Japan Governor Ueda, after raising rates by 25bps to 
1.25% in September, alluded to rising inflation pressures, 
stating the policy environment has shifted to a “new phase”. 
He added that the BoJ must now act to prevent inflation from 
overshooting, rather than simply trying to reach the 2% target. 
September’s BoJ decision came in a 7-2 split vote, with two 
members nominated by Prime Minister Takaichi calling for a 
hold. However, Ueda’s post -decision comments lead us to 
believe that the BoJ board will be comfortable with a 25bps 
rate hike every quarter until Q2 2027, taking the rate to 2%.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Our view

Foundation: Fixed income assets are a  core holding (Neutral) in our portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an 
Overweight stance on EM USD government bonds and an Underweight stance on DM government bonds.  
The Fed’s decision to hike rates in the past week confirms our expectations of a central bank willing to establish its independence 
against political pressure. The Fed's moderately hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. We expect Fed 
to hike twice, one by year-end and another hike in H1 2027 to  bring the Fed funds rate to 4.5%. The price reaction in the US 
government bond yield curve le ads us to believe the short -to-middle part of the curve ha s factored in most of the rate 
expectations. We now prefer to add positions in the 3-7-year bond maturity bucket. As oil price moderates in H2 2027, we see 
room for the Fed to contemplate rate cuts. That would also enable 10-year yields to come down, alongside the front end of the 
curve. Meanwhile, solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep yield premiums tight, even if the scope 
for further compression is limited. In EM, we continue to favour EM USD over local currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on European bank additional tier-1 (AT1) bonds (contingent convertibles [CoCos1]; FX-
hedged), US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, US utilities corporate hybrids, broad US HY and AUD corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We are Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, the 
Fed's policy pivot this month followed Chair Warsh's hawkish 
Jackson Hole speech and recent economic data prints, 
including a pick-up in job creation and inflation over the summer 
and a sharp rebound in oil prices due to the re-escalation of the 
Middle East conflict. Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike 
rates this week, we expect two more hikes by June 2027 on the 
back of robust growth amid strong AI investments and a 
balanced labour market, keeping inflation elevated.  
Over the next few months, we expect the 2-, 10- and 30-year 
US government bond yields to remain rangebound, with the 
10-year yield trading in the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons 
for this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially 
rising further due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle 
East; 2) AI capex plans showing no signs of slowing down, 
which continues to drive above -trend growth in the economy 
and AI -related heavy bond issuance. While short -covering 
trades could move bond yields lower temporarily as net-short 
positions in US government bond futures have reached 
extreme levels, we think such downshifts would be short-lived. 
By H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate and the 
inflationary effects from tariffs to fade, allowing the Fed to 
contemplate rate cuts. US 30 -year yields, on the other hand, 
are expected to remain elevated as the US fiscal deficit 
remains substantial next year. In addition, we do not think the 
so-called ‘Bessent put’  - expanding purchases of long -dated 
bonds, while skewing new issuance towards shorter 
maturities - will be effective as this does not address the 
fundamental issues  of rising debt levels . These factors are 
likely to keep the 'term premium' elevated. 
In the Euro area and Japan, the ECB and BoJ also hiked rates 
this month. It is the first time on record all three - Fed, ECB 
and BoJ - have hiked in the same month, as they respond 
primarily to a resurgence in energy -driven inflation amid tight 
job markets. BoJ is expected to continue hiking once every 
quarter till mid -2027 as we believe the bank  is behind the 
curve and will need to speed up the pace of rate hikes.

Global Market Outlook 10 
DM corporates – Core holding  
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across developed market credit. Yield premiums have 
bounced but remain close to historical lows, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability, and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Fig. 12 AI issuance challenges IG technical backdrop 
AI issuance (total & share of total), US IG gross issuance

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
particularly in the IG space, where heavy issuance related to 
AI continues to weigh on the long end of the curve. AI -driven 
supply is approaching USD 220bn for 2026  in US IG  and 
accounting for an increasingly significant share of gross 
issuance and crowding out other long-dated maturity bonds. 
We maintain a preference for selectivity at the long end, 
favouring the front to belly of the curve ( 3-7 years maturity) 
where risk -adjusted carry remains most durable. Issuance -
driven concessions in sectors with strong capital expenditure 
cycles (notably technology, capital goods, and basic 
industries) present opportunities for investors who can 
stomach near term volatility, especially when fund flows have 
remained robust: so far this year, high grade funds have seen 
cumulative inflows of ~8% of AUM. 
Fig. 13 DM IG spreads have bounced lately, but remain 
near historical lows 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
As an income proposition, DM HY continues to be well 
supported, with all-in yields in the mid -7% range and default 
rate still below trend for US HY in 2026. The shorter duration 
profile of HY, combined with its lower sensitivity to term 
premium and rate volatility, makes it an attractive sleeve in the 
current phase of the cycle. Despite experiencing net outflow 
YTD, HY demand has recovered from a net outflow position 
in the past 1-2 months, underscoring broad investor appetite 
for carry strategies even as spreads remain tight. We remain 
attentive to evolving rating migration trends and sector -
specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades slow and 
new issuance clusters around key theme s. However, we  
continue to see HY bonds as core holding allocations driven 
by healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics. 
Fig. 14 DM HY spreads are near historical lows as well 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight USD, 
core holding for local currency (LCY) bonds 
We re tain an  Overweight position in EM USD government 
bonds and retain EM LCY government bonds as a Core 
holding, reflecting a balanced perspective across both 
regional performance and currency dynamics. In EM USD 
sovereign debt, periods of spread widening have been brief  
and shallow, with attractive carry drawing sustained demand 
amid resilient global growth and stable external conditions. 
Issuance patterns remain front -loaded across many regions, 
reinforcing supportive technicals; relatively light investor 
positioning con tributes to contained risk of unwinding and 
limits market impact during episodes of moderate outflow. 
Regional nuances are increasingly notable. Latin America’s 
sovereign debt has tended to outperform, supported by more 
proactive monetary policy responses and firmer external 
balances. In Asia, higher -rated issuers offer valuable buffer 
during bouts of glob al rate volatility, while select frontier 
exposures within Africa and EMEA remain more sensitive to 
liquidity shifts and changes in risk appetite. Index composition 
also shapes resilience: the EM USD benchmark is less 
exposed to oil price shocks as non -oil importers represent a 
large share of exposure compared to the LCY universe. These 
features are particularly important as spreads across EM USD 
sovereigns hover near historically tight levels, leaving limited 
room for error. We expect returns to be primarily driven by 
carry rather than further spread compression.

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

We are Overweight global equities as we expect solid earnings growth to drive the market higher . The wave of central 
bank rate hikes in September represent a near-term headwind to equity valuations, but these have been largely priced into 
markets by the rise in bond yields year-to-date. We expect earnings growth to offset valuation headwinds. We are Overweight 
US and AxJ equities, where AI adoption continues to drive growth in the technology and adjacent sectors. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, with good visibility on AI-driven growth. We lower China to a Core holding, 
as the valuation re-rating potential appears delayed. Within AxJ, we maintain core allocations to South Korea and India, and we 
are Underweight on ASEAN equities. 
We maintain a Core allocation to Japan, as nominal growth supports broad earnings revision, and a Core allocation to Europe 
ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings inflexion. We remain Underweight UK equities, which lacks exposure to growth sectors.

8 Equity – at a glance

Sundeep Gantori, CFA, CAIA 
Chief Investment Officer, Equities 
Fook Hien Yap 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 13

Global Market Outlook 13

Expansion at a solid pace  
With economic activity expanding at a solid pace , we expect 
earnings growth to fundamentally drive global equities higher. 
Heading into 2027, rising AI adoption continues to drive 
strong growth  in the technology sector. Recent debates 
about regulating the pace of model developments could 
influence the mix of AI spending, with differing impacts on 
various parts of the AI value chain. However, rising adoption 
and growth in AI use cases should support massive growth in 
capex, benefitting the semiconductor industry, cloud service 
providers and internet platforms. The capex benefits also 
spillover to other industries providing the power infrastructure, 
materials and financing of the investmen ts. The risks are a 
slowdown in AI capex or net job losses from AI disruption, but 
we do not see these having a significant impact yet. 
Rate hikes  from central banks represent a headwind for 
equity valuations, although this has been largely priced in by 
bond markets, given the rise in yields we have seen this year. 
Further rate hikes are being priced by the market. However, 
we expect these headwinds to be offset by solid earnings 
growth in global equities: at 34% in 2026 and 16% in 2027.  
Fig. 17 Global equities continue to be supported by 
strong earnings growth, led by the technology sector  
Earnings growth by sectors for MSCI AC World index

Source: Company reports, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have softened this year  as strong 
earnings growth have outpaced the rise in the market. The 
12m forward P/E of around 20x remains at a premium to other 
markets but justified, in our view, by the high returns on equity, 
differences in sector composition and superior growth outlook. 
In the run-up to US mid-term elections in November, we would 
not be surprised to see higher volatility, though we would use 
any undue corrections as opportunities to add exposure. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ enjoys the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key 
beneficiary of the AI capex cycle. AxJ also has the lowest 
P/E valuation, given the earnings surge ha s not yet been  
reflected in the market. Within AxJ, we are Overweight 
Taiwan, which benefits from clear visibility on AI -driven 
growth and dominance in manufacturing advanced AI chips. 
We lower China to a Core holding as the valuation re -
rating potential now appears delayed. Share placements for 
AI spending will likely continue, diluting earnings. Regulatory 
concerns still linger from US restrictions on AI chips and 
distillation accusations, even as the Trump-Xi summit signals 
a mutual desire for steady ties. Looming offshore trust taxes 
may lead to further sell down pressures , while domestic 
demand remains weak. However, valuation s remain 
inexpensive with rapid tech innovation and AI -localisation 
investments. Policy stimulus could provide upside. 
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are 
positive on the long-term agreements giving greater earnings 
visibility on memory chip s. Positioning is also cleaner, with 
reduced foreign investor exposure and declin ing leveraged 
ETFs. India remains a Core holding amid positive earnings 
revision and strong domestic demand , alongside  macro 
headwinds from RBI rate hikes. We remain Underweight on 
ASEAN, as oil shocks and a supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 18 Global equity valuations have declined this year, 
with market gains led by earnings growth 
Composition of returns YTD for regional MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Standard Chartered. Min-Max over last 10 years 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal economic growth is supporting solid 
broad-based earnings revisions , with rising rates 
supportive of bank earnings. The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is also pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings inflection  after three 
stagnant years, with rising rates supportive of banks’ 
earnings. Fiscal expansion is benefitting domestic demand 
and the industrial sector, although earnings growth in the 
region still lags that of the US and AxJ.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK , 
making it lag the performance of other markets.

Global Market Outlook 14

Add Global Semiconductors, remove HSTECH 
We initiate d an Opportunistic Idea on Global 
Semiconductors on 9 Sep t 2026. We expect sustained AI 
investments and rising AI adoption to support earnings 
growth, with demand widening beyond processors to memory, 
networking, and advanced manufacturing equipment . 
Valuations have also eased since the middle of the year. 
We remove Hang Seng Technology (31 Oct 2024 to 24 Sep 
2026: -1.2%) amid mixed earnings trends, while AI 
monetisation has yet to keep pace with rising investments. 
Widening US-China tech restrictions adds to the uncertainty, 
and we see better risk-adjusted opportunities elsewhere.

Ongoing Opportunistic Ideas 
Euro area banks: A higher-for-longer rates backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening  
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income  is growing . Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Global Power & Electrification: Rising AI and data centre 
power demand should drive a multi -year grid and 
electrification investment cycle. Power availability and grid 
capacity are emerging bottlenecks to the AI infra buildout. 
Addressing these constraints requires capex and time, which 
supports strong earnings visibility for grid equipment makers. 
Japan Banks : BoJ policy normali sation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver  independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
MSCI Taiwan: Taiwan’s foundry leadership makes it a critical 
supplier to global tech firms, while its broadening AI hardware 
supply chain is driving strong revenue growth.  
Global High Dividend  payers: Provides a higher -income 
equity allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI 
ACWI and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
US Communication Services: We remain constructive and 
expect further upward earnings revisions, driven by improving 
digital ad-spending and AI monetisation. 
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We upgrade US Materials to Overweight as robust capex 
drives demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials, while we remain bullish on gold . US Technology 
and Communication Services remain the primary growth 
engines, supported by structural AI tailwinds. We remain 
Overweight in Financials across the US, Europe ex -UK, and 
Japan, underpinned by earnings stability  and elevated rates 
supporting interest income. This is further supported by a US 
capital markets recovery  and European balance -sheet 
strength. We remain Overweight in Europe ex-UK Industrials, 
reflecting EU fiscal stimulus and Germany's infrastructure 
acceleration legislation. 
In China, we broaden our Overweight exposure by upgrading 
three sectors: Materials (benefiting from structurally elevated 
commodity prices), Healthcare (biotech innovation and a robust 
pipeline sustaining growth), and Financials (easing margin 
pressure, fee recovery, and capital market reform). Our existing 
Overweight positio ns in Technology and Communication 
Services maintain exposure to China's AI build-out, with fiscal 
deployment into high-tech infrastructure serving as a near-term 
catalyst. On the other hand, we downgrade Utilities to 
Underweight on capped margins and retain our Underweight 
views on Real Estate and Consumer Staples.

Gold outlook:  Gold has remained resilient despite a more 
hawkish Fed and higher real yields, suggesting much of the 
tightening is already reflected in prices. With markets 
discounting an aggressive Fed path, we see an increasingly 
asymmetric risk -reward: limited downsid e risk from further 
rate repricing, but meaningful upside should Fed rate 
expectations shift towards being less restrictive. Official -
sector demand has slowed from recent years but has become 
broader based, while investor positioning remains well below 
levels seen at the start of the 2022 hiking cycle. 
Oil outlook: Oil prices have risen sharply as recent attacks 
cast greater uncertainty over Saudi export s, even as Gulf 
crude flows overall remain resilient. Other regional producers 
have helped sustain exports, containing the immediate supply 
impact. However, adequate US commercial crude stocks 
mask thinner buffers elsewhere, with strategic reserves and 
fuel inventories low. This leaves the m arket more vulnerable 
to a prolonged disruption, supporting a higher geopolitical risk 
premium and an upward revision to our outlook.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 16

Global Market Outlook 16

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

EUR/USD 1.1380 1.15 1.18 
Near-term EUR weakness; constructive medium term. The ECB raised its deposit 
rate 25bps to 2.50% in September. Potential ECB tightening should narrow the US-
Euro area rate differential over time, supporting EUR over 12 months.

GBP/USD 1.3219 1.33 1.35 
Rangebound with near term downside risk: UK inflation rose to 3.1% y/y, keeping 
BoE tightening risk alive, but softer labour conditions should limit the extent of 
tightening. Fed -BoE policy divergence may constrain GBP near term, while an 
expected peak in US rates by mid -2027 should allow a modes t recovery over the 
medium term. The Autum Budget in October could increase volatility.

USD/JPY 158.86 157 153 
Constructive JPY. The BoJ's September 25bp hike to 1.25% reinforces policy 
normalisation. The still wide  US-Japan yield differential may limit near-term JPY 
gains, but further BoJ tightening should gradually narrow the gap and support JPY 
over the medium term towards our target of 153.

AUD/USD 0.7012 0.73 0.74 
Near-term AUD weakness; constructive medium term: Australia’s Q2 GDP grew 
0.4% q/q, reinforcing domestic resilience. A relatively restrictive RBA stance supports 
AUD, while eventual narrowing in relative rate differentials should improve the 
medium-term outlook.

NZD/USD 0.5662 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: The RBNZ raised rates 25bps to 2.75%, while New Zealand’s Q2 
GDP grew 0.2% q/q. Improving growth provides support, but an uneven recovery and 
near-term USD strength should limit NZD gains.

USD/CAD 1.4138 1.39 1.37 
Near-term CAD weakness; modest medium-term recovery: Canada employment 
fell 41,700 in August vs. a 15,000 expected rise, while the BoC held rates at 2.25%. 
Softer labour conditions and Fed-BoC divergence may constrain CAD near term.

USD/CHF 0.8278 0.82 0.79 
Near-term CHF weakness; medium-term CHF recovery: Swiss inflation surprised 
higher at 0.8% y/y, but the wide Fed -SNB rate differential should continue to favour 
USD near term. As Fed tightening peaks and the US rate advantage narrows, CHF 
should gradually recover over 12 months.

USD/CNH 6.7159 6.70 6.67 
Gradual CNH appreciation: China’s industrial output in August rose 5.2% y/y, 
beating estimates, but retail sales growth slowed to 0.4% on weak domestic demand. 
Production resilience and PBoC currency support favour modest CNH appreciation, 
while soft consumption and an easing policy bias should limit the pace of gains.

USD/SGD 1.2794 1.28 1.26 
Rangebound near term; constructive SGD medium term: Further Fed tightening 
and elevated US yields may limit SGD appreciation near term. Resilient 
fundamentals and MAS's exchange -rate policy should support gradual SGD 
appreciation as US rate support fades.

USD/INR 95.96 – 98 
Modestly bearish INR: India’s crude oil imports rose 26% y/y in August, and a USD 
77bn AI import deficit are both adding to structural import pressures. Higher -for-
longer US rates remain a headwind, while capital inflows and RBI intervention should 
smooth out near-term volatility and support a gradual INR depreciation towards 98.

USD/MYR 4.0865 – 4.00 Modestly bullish MYR: Resilient domestic demand, strong exports and corporate FX conversion support MYR; external uncertainty should keep appreciation gradual.

USD/KRW 1,368.9 – 1,540 
Bearish KRW: Recent KRW -supportive corporate USD selling is fading, while 
reduced National Pension Service’s FX hedging and prospect of USD 350bn US 
investment package by the South Korean national investment committee could 
increase USD demand. Higher US rates add a he adwind, supporting KRW 
depreciation over 12 months.

### Page 18

Global Market Outlook 18

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19 
Fig. 32 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

### Page 24

Global Market Outlook 24

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is

portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family.

 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 25

Global Market Outlook 25

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

## Document 287: First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ household incomes, assets and savings update - June quarter 2026 (2026-10-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#consumer #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ household incomes, assets and savings
update - June quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
10:24 October 08 2026 Share
New Zealand incomes and savings are rising, as is the value of households’ financial
assets. However, the value of housing assets remains flat.
Key takeouts from Stats NZ’s latest update on household incomes, assets and savings (June quarter
2026)

### Page 2

Household incomes
Disposable income levels in the economy grew by 5.2% in the year to June, down from 6.5% in the
year to March

Savings and wealth
Household savings have continued to rise, climbing by $2.5b over the past quarter.

Overall household wealth levels were up 1.9% over the past year. Under the surface, the make-up of
household wealth continues to shift.

Interest costs 
Overall household income levels are continuing to rise. Much of that is due to increases in
entrepreneurial earnings (which is in part due to increased earnings in the agricultural sector). Wage
and salary growth has been more limited but has picked up.
Household savings levels have been rising for three years now, with another solid rise over the past
three months. 
Overall household wealth levels have risen modestly. Although the value of households’ financial
assets is continuing to rise, housing assets have essentially shown no growth since late 2023.
Household spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter. That’s the first increase in
two years. The easing in average borrowing costs seen over the past few years has come to a close
with the RBNZ’s hiking cycle now in train.
Underpinning the rise in overall income levels have been continued increases in entrepreneurial
earnings, which are up 12% over the past year. That will include stronger earnings in the agricultural
sector, which has been boosted by the strength in commodity export prices. That lift in agricultural
earnings has been reflected in stronger economic confidence and spending in many rural regions.
We expect commodity export earnings will remain firm over the year ahead. 
In contrast, wage and salary earnings are up just 2.7% over the past year. That’s up from 1.8% in the
year to March, but it’s a fairly muted rise, especially given the continued increases in the population
and inflation of 4.1%.
June’s large increase in savings builds on the solid increases seen over the past three years.
The savings rate in the June quarter was 3.7% of disposable incomes, down just slightly from last
quarter.
The value of households’ financial assets has continued to rise and is up 4.6% over the past year. 
However, the value of housing and land assets is down 0.9% over the past year, and it has remained
essentially unchanged since late 2023. We expect housing market conditions will remain soft over
the coming year, limiting increases in overall household wealth. 
Households’ spending on interest costs rose just slightly in the June quarter, climbing by 0.3%.
While that’s a relatively small change, it is the first increase that we’ve seen in two years.

### Page 3

Related articles

Media Contact:
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist
P: +64 9 336 5668
M: +64 21 710 852
Over the past few years, many borrowers saw their debt servicing costs fall as they rolled off earlier
high mortgage rates and onto lower ones. That helped to support household spending.
However, the easing in average borrowing cost has now ended. Recent months have seen mortgage
rates pushing higher as the RBNZ has begun to hike the Official Cash Rate, with further hikes
expected over the coming year. 
Looking ahead, we don’t expect a significant increase in average household borrowing costs.
However, we won’t see the same support for spending that we did over the past few years.

We expect high bond supply in 2027 for utilities due to redemptions and high

We forecast European utilities to raise

2027 is likely to be another eventful year for European utilities.
Geopolitical uncertainty, volatility in energy markets and
signiﬁcant investment pipelines, together with the associated
funding needs, should remain key drivers for the sector going into
next year.
We forecast €75bn of total bond supply from investment-grade
utilities in 2027, only slightly below the €80bn we have pencilled in
for full-year 2026. Net supply also remains ﬁrmly positive at €35bn

next year, although somewhat below the €44bn in 2026.
Utilities bond supply set to stay elevated
with high capex and redemptions
Realised and forecast euro-denominated bond issuance vs redemptions
from investment grade utilities

Corporate hybrids remain an important part
of the funding mix
Utilities have raised €19bn with corporate hybrids so far in 2026,
the highest level since 2015. Hybrid issuance helps protect the
credit proﬁles of these companies as they ﬁnance their extensive

investment pipelines with debt alongside cash ﬂows.
While we expect corporate hybrids to remain an important part of
the funding mix of utilities in 2027, the choice between senior and

High capital expenditure drives supply
Alongside redemptions, bond supply continues to be driven by
large capital expenditure. We expect combined investment to top
€200bn next year for large European utility issuers. German names

have the highest investment needs for 2027, up around 30% year-
on-year, while Italian, UK, and Dutch names are also among those
seeing a clear increase.
The energy transition continues to drive signiﬁcant investment
needs, with growing electriﬁcation and renewable generation
requiring substantial expansion and reinforcement of transmission
and distribution grids. Regulated utilities are particularly impacted
by higher grid investments, but integrated utilities are also likely to

see higher capital expenditure next year.
Higher interest rates may make some companies more selective in
their investments. The bulk of these programmes is likely to
proceed, however, as the acute investment needs of many network
utilities are unlikely to change any time soon. Potential execution

## Document 355: Sustainable bank bond supply set for further growth in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sustainable bank bond supply set for further growth in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Sustainable bank bond supply is close to reaching a record in
2026, despite Europe’s ESG simpliﬁcation agenda. We expect
another increase to €100bn in 2027, supported by sustainable
lending growth, higher redemptions and resilient investor
demand
Maureen Schuller
Sustainable bank bond supply is blooming, nearing a record in 2026
Banks issued an unexpectedly large amount of sustainable debt
this year despite Europe’s ESG deregulation eﬀorts. Up to mid-

October 2026, they issued more than €86bn in sustainable bonds
across diﬀerent instrument types, up by almost €20bn from the
same period last year.
This was a surprisingly strong increase given that favourable
primary market conditions oﬀered issuers little greenium, despite
continued evidence that sustainable bonds attract higher investor
demand, as reﬂected in stronger oversubscription rates. This
suggests that the availability of sustainable assets was not a

Cookie settings
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
Here you can specify which type of cookies you consent to and

please conﬁrm your preferences. You can change your cookie

Conﬁrm selection Accept all

### Page 2

meaningful constraint on banks' sustainable debt issuance, thanks
to higher redemptions and sustainable lending growth.
Issuers are also increasingly directing ESG bond issuance towards
European capital markets, often at the expense of North American
markets, reﬂecting the growing divergence in political and
regulatory priorities between the two regions.
The bulk of the YTD supply (€76.6bn) (re)ﬁnanced green assets, with
green supply up €25bn from 2025. Instead, issuance in social and

sustainability formats did not exceed €9.6bn, trailing last year’s
supply by €4.5bn. All instrument types, from secured to unsecured
to subordinated, contributed to the higher ESG supply volumes.
However, sustainable T2 and bail-in senior (senior non-preferred
and HoldCo) issuance increased particularly strongly, as the
funding cost advantage from sustainable issuance was better for
these instruments than for covered bonds and preferred senior
debt.

Issuance under the European Green Bond Standard (EuGBS) rose
from €2.5bn to almost €12bn YTD. For many banks, ICMA-aligned
green bond issuance remained the more practical option as it
allows them to ﬁnance a broader pool of assets, rather than fully
commit the bond proceeds to Taxonomy-aligned activities. This is
particularly relevant when non-EU assets are ﬁnanced, for which
the EU regulation-based do-no-signiﬁcant-harm requirements
(DNSH) are diﬃcult to apply.

That said, with the revisions to the EU Taxonomy’s technical
screening criteria around the corner (expected in 4Q 2026), we
expect more banks to inaugurate European Green Bond
Programmes, as more types of green assets will become available
due to the EU’s simpliﬁcation agenda.
Sustainable bank bond supply will reach
€100bn in 2027

What is driving growth in ESG supply in
2027?
We expect sustainable bank bond supply to reach €100bn in
FY2027, up from €93bn in FY2026 and equivalent to 22% of total
euro-denominated bank bond supply. Green issuance remains
dominant at €85bn, of which €17bn (20%) will be issued under the
EU Green Bond Standard (EuGBS). Social, sustainable and
sustainability-linked supply will total €15bn, of which 80% will be

issued in social format.
Greater availability of social assets, together with regulatory
initiatives such as the Aﬀordable Housing Act presented by the
European Commission in September 2026, should help social
issuance to gain further traction in 2027.
Green supply will reach €85bn of which
20% will be issued as EuGBs

Includes only euro-denominated bank bonds with a minimum size of

Source: IGM, ING
Sustainable loan origination will, in general, remain an important
driver of ESG supply. Higher interest rate levels will dampen lending
growth in 2027, but sustainable lending will likely be impacted to a
lesser extent.
Borrowing for energy eﬃciency improvements or with a low carbon
footprint could trim borrowing costs, while high energy prices and
costs may incentivise companies and households to move forward

with investments to reduce their energy dependence and use.
Banks’ net-zero commitments also support the continued focus on
the greening of their balance sheets.
Moreover, banks will repay €67bn in EUR sustainable debt in 2027
up from €54bn in 2026. These redemptions should release some
assets for reﬁnancing, although loan amortisation, look-back
periods and changes in use-of-proceeds criteria may prevent a full
reﬁnancing with new sustainable debt. In 2026, banks replaced

70% of their sustainable redemptions partially or fully with new
sustainable supply. A comparable trend for 2027 would already
cover close to half of our supply estimate for next year.

Besides these fundamental supply drivers, periods of geopolitical or
sovereign debt market volatility could encourage banks to apply a
green or social label to a larger share of their issuance. This may
help them broaden and diversify their investor base and support
the execution of their transactions.
EU simpliﬁcation and asset-identiﬁcation
developments
The EU’s eﬀorts to simplify ESG rules will have mixed implications

for the growth of sustainable asset pools. The reduced corporate
sustainability reporting scope may impede asset identiﬁcation,
while revisions to the Taxonomy’s technical screening criteria could
support the growth of eligible green assets.
This year’s narrowing of the scope of the EU Corporate
Sustainability Reporting Directive (CSRD) to companies with more
than 1,000 employees and a turnover above €450m makes it more
diﬃcult for European banks to identify assets on their balance

sheets that are suitable for sustainable bond issuance. The
simpliﬁcation of the European sustainability reporting standards
(ESRS) and the value chain cap add to this challenge.
However, the voluntary reporting standards applicable from 1
January 2027 could still provide banks with some essential data on
smaller companies in their value chain. This may include

### Page 6

identiﬁcation, the proposed revisions to the Taxonomy’s technical
screening criteria could support green portfolio growth once they
enter into force in early 2027. For instance, the substantial
contribution criteria for green buildings constructed before 2027
remain mostly unchanged. The criteria would also be expanded to
include buildings that achieve a reduction in primary energy
demand of at least 60%. Combined with the streamlining of DNSH
criteria, this could support the expansion of Taxonomy-aligned

green portfolios.
The technical screening criteria for buildings built after 1 January
2027 will converge more closely with the revised European Energy
Performance of Buildings Directive (EPBD 2024/1275). However, they
would already subject all new buildings to the Zero Emission
Building (ZEB) criterion and life-cycle global warming potential
(GWP) calculations ahead of the EPBD’s 1 January 2030 deadline.
Additional recommendations from the Platform on Sustainable

Finance could also make some criteria more restrictive. The
Platform has recommended introducing a stricter top-15%
benchmark for buildings constructed before 2021, based on 2023-
26 building stock data, with the threshold tightening every ﬁve
years to align with the trajectory towards net-zero by 2050. If
incorporated into the ﬁnal technical screening criteria, this

### Page 7

Regulation (SFDR) should also remain supportive of demand for
sustainable bonds, even though these revisions will probably not
apply before 2029 (i.e. two years after implementation).
The SFDR review is set to enter trilogue negotiations, with the
Council agreeing on its position in June 2026 and the Parliament
adopting its negotiating position in September 2026. The European
Commission published its ﬁrst proposals to revise the SFDR in
November 2025, providing for three new product categories to

The transition category covers products that invest in companies
or assets with a credible path towards sustainability or that pursue
certain environmental or social transition-related objectives. The
ESG basics category includes products that integrate other
sustainability considerations beyond sustainability risks in their
investment strategy. The sustainable category encompasses
products investing in companies or assets that already meet high
sustainability standards or pursue a particular environmental or

social objective.
For each category, a proposed minimum of 70% of the investments
should be made according to the respective sustainability claim.
For the transition and sustainable categories, the 70% condition
would also be met if the proportion of investments in Taxonomy-
aligned activities is at least 15%. This would support demand
among investors subject to the SFDR for bonds that ﬁnance
Taxonomy-aligned activities.
The applicable Paris-aligned benchmark (PAB) exclusions do not

apply to use-of-proceeds instruments such as European green

Simpliﬁcation won't stall banks'
sustainable supply
Europe’s simpliﬁcation drive is not derailing sustainable bank
bond supply. Growing sustainable loan portfolios, higher
redemptions and resilient investor demand should support
further growth in 2027, even as overall bank bond supply
remains broadly stable.

《消費量能未見失速訊號》
• 8月總體市場消費數據偏保守，
然維持正向表現
1
資產 4Q26 看法 投資展望
核心消費 中立 從已公告的核心消費個股展望，本行預期今年上下半年營收成長48：52
的溫和成長預期，同時零售商備貨行為與終端消費需求仍支持到貨量的
基本盤，但消費者實際支出意願可能受到油價／利率上升抑制，導致平
均單價、毛利率受到一定壓縮，尤其在低、中所得族群及易受油價影響
的旅遊／實體零售類別。4Q26預計呈「量能可望維持、獲利偏弱」格局
資料來源：美國銀行2026/9/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/18。

• 消費量能未見失速訊號 P.1
• 「K型」分化持續收斂，低所得消
費動能並非來自舉債 P.2
• 重塑消費環境與消費類股估值基礎 P.3

消費產業投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/21
本行長期追蹤美國銀行信用卡消費統計數據(Consumer
checkpoimt，月報) 顯示，8月消費動能自7月疲弱中明顯
反彈。從美國銀行發行的信用卡、簽帳卡合計消費YoY成
長率已連續三個月降溫（6月6.3%→7月5.0%→8月4.5%）
剔除油品後同步走緩（5.6%→4.3%→3.7%），主因6月世
界盃與線上促銷時序錯位帶來的高基期效應消退。惟季調
後MoM動能出現反轉，7月一度下滑（-0.2%），8月明顯
回升（+0.9%）。商務部數據同步佐證：8月零售銷售季調
後月增1.2%，優於市場預期0.7%，成長動能來自線上零售、
加油站（價格推升）及餐飲、電子產品等類別。

2
《「K型」分化持續收斂，低所得消費並非來自舉債》
8 月非必需消費 YoY成長 ， 高所得 （ 5.9% ） 與低所得
（5.7%）差距僅0.2個百分點 ，為2024年1月以來最小；
中所得 （5.1%）略為落後 。低所得族群稅後工資成長已
連續第二個月領先其他所得層。
另紐約聯準銀行資料顯示 ，2026年第二季家庭總負債為

18.771兆美元 ，季減130億美元 ，年增3,830億美元；信
用卡餘額季增210億美元、年增540億美元至1.263兆美元，
負債溫和成長，並非急遽加槓桿。嚴重違約（90天以上）
的 年 化 轉 出 率 方 面， 信 用 卡 由 去 年 同 期6.93% 微升至
6.97%。這與美銀 Checkpoint 揭露的另一項正面訊號互
相呼應：所有所得層級「每月全額繳清信用卡帳單」的家
庭比例都在上升，僅繳最低應繳金額的比例雖也上升但幅
度較小。綜合來看，原先市場擔憂的低所得者會藉由舉債
消費，但目前數據並不支持這樣的看法 ，等於是提供K型
收斂假說的有力證據
《重塑消費環境與消費類股估值基礎》
9月上旬發生兩項關鍵變化 ，根本性重塑消費環境與消費
類股估值基礎：聯準會於 9/16啟動三年多來首次升息 （1
碼至3.75%-4.00%），新任主席沃許（Kevin Warsh）強
調通膨仍偏高 ，點陣顯示年內可能再加 1碼；同時密西根
大學消費者信心指數9月初值急跌至47.8，創自1978年以

來次低。
資料來源：美國銀行 2026/9/15，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/18

BofA全球基金經理人調查（GFMS，9月15日發布）顯示，
投資人現金持有比率上升至3.9%（8月3.5%），綜合情緒
指數回落至7.0（8月8.0），股票配置從淨超配56%降至
49%；區域面，投資人對消費類持續極端低配——必需消
費品（Staples）淨低配33%，為2004年1月以來最大，
非必需消費品（Discretionary）淨低配21%，相對於金
融、科技、健康照護等淨超配部門形成鮮明對比。這代表
消費類股籌碼面已獲得沉澱。
《4Q26傳統銷售旺季Bottom-Up需求強弱度分析》

由於NRF尚未發布2026年假期旺季（11-12月）專屬銷售
預測（歷年慣例於10-11月發布），且目前可用最新官方
預測為發生在中東戰爭之前。因此改採bottom-up方式，
以供需鏈前置指標、零售商財測、消費者信心與成本端壓
力四大面向交叉驗證。
➢ NRF Global Port Tracker預估9月將為全年主要貨櫃港
口進口量最高的月份，異於往年7-8月峰期的季節性規
律，顯示零售商對4Q旺季需求仍持樂觀，提前備貨以
應對可能的關稅／運輸不確定性。
➢ 零售商財測訊號分化，Target（同比銷售+3.8%，營收
+5.3%，全年EPS指引上調至$9.90-10.90）與Best
Buy（同比銷售+4.1%，全年指引上調至營收$42.3-
42.8bn，同店銷售1.9-3%）均上調全年指引。
資料來源：密西根大學2026/9，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/18

4
，反映電子／電腦、家居、美妝、新品類需求扎實。相較
之下，Walmart雖也上調全年指引 ，但Q3單季指引轉軟
（EPS $0.62-0.64，同店銷售3.0-3.75%），股價在公布
後重挫9%——市場解讀為Q2超預期主要來自一次性關稅
退款效應（約750個基點的毛利貢獻 ），而非需求結構性
改善 ，該退款已大幅讓利投入價格競爭 （單季推出超過

1.1萬項降價），意味著Q2的優異表現部分係提前透支Q3。
同時電商與數位通路持續為成長引擎： Walmart全球電商
+23% 、 Target數位同店 +8.7% 、 Home Depot 線上
+11%（連續五季雙位數成長 ）、Lowe's線上+15.7%，
顯示全通路投資持續帶動業績。
➢ 消費者「精打細算」特徵明顯：off-price（折扣）業態
如TJX、Ross Stores同店成長強勁（分別+4%、
+10%），反映消費者尋求高性價比商品的行為持續強
化。
➢ 關稅退款效應將於Q3起逐漸消退，市場焦點將轉向排
除一次性利益後的核心毛利率與同店銷售動能是否可持
續。
➢ 燃油與運費成本為共同逆風：各公司財報及法說會均提
及油價、運輸成本上升對下半年毛利率的壓力。
結論：4Q26傳統旺季需求呈現「量能可望維持、獲利
品質面臨分化壓力」的中性格局，與兩個月前「消費持續
熱絡」的單一樂觀敘事相比，風險報酬比已明顯向下傾斜
(亦即產業獲利成長動能趨緩)，整體消費的評價面難有向
上提升的動力來源。

## Document 560: 回復するドイツ経済、高まる国民の不満 ～成長率見通しは上方修正も、自律回復と構造改革に高い壁～

Source PDF: all report pdf/回復するドイツ経済、高まる国民の不満 ～成長率見通しは上方修正も、自律回復と構造改革に高い壁～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月９日

1/4 
回復するドイツ経済、高まる国民の不満 
～成長率見通しは上方修正も、自律回復と構造改革に高い壁～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ ドイツ政府は2026・2027 年の成長率予測を１％台前半に上方修正した。 財政拡張と輸出の回
復が景気を下支えする一方、個人消費や設備投資の低迷が続き、自律的な景気回復には至っ
ていない。 財政刺激が一巡する2028 年には再び低成長に陥る恐れがあり、 持続的な成長軌道
への復帰には構造改革が不可欠だ。しかし、景気回復の恩恵が国民生活の改善につながって
おらず、政府の改革方針に対する不信感が高まっている。州議会選挙での相次ぐ与党敗北も
政権の求心力を低下させており、経済再生に必要な改革を実行できるかが問われている。

■成長率見通しを上方修正も、景気回復の基盤は脆弱 
過去数年、景気停滞が続いてきたドイツ経済に回復の兆しが広がっている。2023 年にマイナス
成長に転落、2024 年もゼロ成長、2025 年も＋0.3％の成長にとどまったが、2026 年に入って景気
が上向いている（図表１） 。こうしたなか、ドイツ政府が８日に公表した経済見通しでは、2026 年
の実質国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）成長率を従来の＋0.5％から＋1.3％に、2027 年を＋0.9％から＋
1.1％に上方修正した。2028 年には＋0.6％へ減速すると予測しており、財政刺激による景気回復
が持続的な成長につながるかは不透明だ。 
今回の上方修正には、2026 年前半の成長率が想定を上回ったことや過去のＧＤＰ統計の遡及改
定が影響している。防衛・インフラ関連の歳出拡大や輸出の底堅さが景気を下支えする一方、個
人消費や設備投資の足取りは鈍く、財政支出に依存しない自律的な景気回復には至っていない。
９月 24 日に公表されたドイツ５大経済研究所の経済見通しも、2026 年と2027 年の成長率をそれ
ぞれ＋1.3％、＋1.1％と予測しており、政府見通しは専門家の予測とも概ね一致している。 
景気の先行きには下振れリスクも残る。足元では水上輸送の大動脈であるライン川の水位低下
が続いており、化学、鉄鋼、エネルギー産業などへの影響が懸念される。中流の観測地点カウプ
の観測水位は10 月８日に３センチ程度まで低下し、貨物船の積載制限やコンテナ輸送への割増料
金の適用など、物流への影響が広がっている。低水位が長期化すれば、原材料・製品輸送の停滞
や物流費の上昇を通じて、工業生産の下押し要因となりかねない。また、イラン情勢を巡る不透
明感が長期化するなか、原油や天然ガス価格の上昇が家計の実質購買力を目減りさせ、企業収益
を圧迫することも懸念される。

### Page 2

2/4

■財政刺激から自律回復へ、構造改革が成長の鍵 
メルツ政権は、防衛費の増額と5000 億ユーロのインフラ特別基金を柱とする財政拡張によって
景気を下支えするとともに、企業の税負担軽減、規制緩和、エネルギー・コストの引き下げ、就
労促進などを通じて、ドイツの産業競争力を回復させる方針だ。ライヒェ経済相も10 月８日の経
済見通しの公表に際して、「景気回復は始まったものの、持続的な成長には民間投資の拡大が不可
欠であり、今後数ヶ月で改革を実行に移す必要がある」と強調した。 
５大研は、今回の景気回復が過去の回復局面と比較して弱く、その背景には高齢化に伴う労働
供給の減少があると指摘している。社会保険料の上昇を抑え、企業の投資意欲を高める抜本的な
構造改革も、現時点では十分に具体化していないとの見方を示している。財政刺激が一巡する
2028 年には成長率が＋0.4％へ減速すると予測しており、これは政府予測の＋0.6％を下回る。財
政拡張によって景気回復の時間を稼ぐことはできても、その間に民間投資の拡大や生産性向上に
つながる改革を進めなければ、持続的な成長力の回復は望みにくい。

■国民生活への恩恵乏しく、改革への不信感が高まる 
景気回復とは裏腹に、国民のメルツ政権に対する評価は厳しい。１日に公表されたＡＲＤの世
出所：欧州統計局資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成
（図表１）ドイツの実質ＧＤＰ成長率（前期比年率）

### Page 3

3/4 
論調査では、メルツ政権の仕事ぶりに「満足している」との回答が10％にとどまった（図表２） 。
８日に公表されたＺＤＦの世論調査では、「政府の改革が正しい方向に進んでいる」と考える人の
割合は30％にとどまり、59％が「誤った方向に進んでいる」と回答した。さらに、80％が「改革
の負担配分が不公平である」と考え、77％が「政府の社会保障制度改革が問題解決に大きく貢献
しない」と回答しており、政府の政策運営に対する不信感が根強い。 
こうした国民の評価と政府の景気判断との隔たりには、景気回復の恩恵が家計に十分に行き渡
っていないことがある。５大研は景気回復が続くにもかかわらず、就業者数が減少すると予測し
ている。政府見通しでも 2026 年の個人消費の伸びは＋0.3％、2027 年も＋0.5％にとどまり、景気
回復が雇用や生活水準の十分な改善につながらないことを示唆している。エネルギー価格の上昇
による家計の実質購買力の圧迫も予想され、国民が景気回復を実感するには時間を要する。加え
て、政府が進める年金・医療・介護などの社会保障制度改革は、将来の負担増や給付の見直しに
つながる。財政拡張による景気の押し上げ効果が国民生活に十分現れる前に、改革に伴う負担へ
の懸念が広がっていることも、政府の政策運営に対する不信感を強める一因と考えられる。

出所：infratest dimap資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所が作成

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4

■政権の求心力低下が構造改革の障害に 
ドイツ経済は財政拡張と輸出の回復によって長期停滞から脱しつつあるが、景気回復の基盤は
なお脆弱だ。エネルギー・コストの高止まり、労働力不足、社会保障負担の増大、産業構造転換
の遅れといった構造問題が解消されなければ、財政刺激の効果が一巡した後、再び低成長に陥る
恐れがある。だが、構造改革の必要性が高まる一方、メルツ政権は州議会選挙での相次ぐ与党敗
北によって求心力を低下させており、改革を進める政治的な環境は厳しさを増している。企業の
競争力回復と家計の負担軽減、財政の持続可能性を両立させることは容易ではなく、社会保障改
革などを巡る負担配分への不満が強まれば、改革が停滞する恐れもある。財政拡張によって景気
回復への道筋は見え始めたが、それを持続的な成長につなげられるかは、国民の理解と支持を得
ながら構造改革を実行できるかにかかっている。

以 上

今年 4 ⽉，宁波联创以 410.34 元 / 股询价转让宁德时代 5800 万股，交易⾦额 238 亿元， 30 家
机构参与接盘。如今股价跌⾄ 237.6 元，接盘⽅浮亏约 42% 。其中 UBS 浮亏约 25 亿元，摩
根⼤通浮亏 17.5 亿元，兴证全球基⾦浮亏近 12 亿元。
摘要：
2026 年 4 ⽉ 23 ⽇，宁德时代（证券代码： 300750 ）股东宁波联创减持，持股⽐例从 6.23% 下降
⾄ 4.96% 。
宁波联创询价转让股份数量为 5800 万股，占公司总股本的 1.27% ；询价转让的价格为 410.34 元 /
股，交易⾦额 238 亿元。
如今，这⼀笔 5800 万股进⼊解禁期。截⾄昨⽇收盘，宁德时代股价为 237.6 元，以收盘价计算，
公司市值为 1.1 万亿。在昨天较前⼀⽇⼤涨 7.8% 的基础上，当初参与接盘宁波联创股权的基⾦依
然浮亏 42% 。
宁德时代过去半年，股价遭遇腰斩。虽然宁德时代昨⽇股价反弹，但有投资者指出，当⼀个赛
道⾼增⻓逻辑不复存在，都会引起恐慌性抛售，每⼀次暴跌后的反弹，都是⼤机构乘机逃跑的

机会。

第三季营收 1652 亿  净利 212 亿
财报显⽰，宁德时代 2026 年前三季营收 4421 亿，同⽐增⻓ 56% ；净利为 645 亿，同⽐增⻓
31.6% ；扣⾮后净利为 578.76 亿，同⽐增⻓ 32.68% 。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
其中，宁德时代 2026 年第三季营收 1652 亿，较上年同期增⻓ 58.58% ；净利 212 亿，同⽐增⻓
14.5% ；扣⾮后净利为 188.6 亿，同⽐增⻓ 14.86% 。
宁德时代⾼管在解读财报时表⽰，第三季度单位盈利能⼒变化有⼏⽅⾯因素：⾸先是产品结构
变化，⼀个季度的产品结构波动不代表⻓期⽅向；第⼆是消费税和出⼝退税的影响。宁德时代
正与客⼾沟通，⼀起来分担相关影响，同时积极推动新技术和新产品的落地，降低对盈利能⼒
的影响。
宁德时代还表⽰，三季度的存货⾦额变⼤是随着公司业务规模发展带来的，但近⼏个季度公司
存货周转天数⼀直相对稳定，并没有销售不畅或出货不畅的情况，属于正常存货周转。从存货
结构来看，增⻓的主要是发出商品，未来会逐渐体现为公司收⼊。在储能⽅⾯，公司也在从销
售电芯和直流侧产品到提供交流侧和系统产品，产品结构和形态较过去更为丰富，但相应的交
付和确认收⼊周期⽐之前有所延⻓。

17.3% ，⻩世霖持股为 9.09% ；

⚫ 2H26 營運仍可維持高檔水準 ，持 續受惠客戶提升份額  
⚫ GaAs 及 InP PD 有望成為第二成長動能，預期於 2028 年量產  
3Q26 營收 略低於 預期 ，毛利率 預期 受原物料成本 呈現下滑 ：公司 3Q26
營收 12. 62 億(QoQ -2.81% ，YoY+ 12.96 %)，略 低於我們 預期， 預估 毛
利率 為 26.14% %(QoQ -3.84 ppts，YoY -2.05 ppts) ，主要受到上游磊晶及
金價持續處於高檔影響，呈現季下滑 ，預估 3Q26 EPS 為 1.07 元。公
司 3Q26 稼動率為 70%~75% ，下一季度將維持相同稼動率水準，預估
4Q26 營收為 12.63 億(QoQ+0.15% ，YoY+0.85%) ， 此外 ， 公司已於3Q26
與大部分客戶商議產品漲價，產品售價調漲將於 4Q26 生效，毛利率
預期將小幅度回升至 27.83%(QoQ+1.69ppts ，YoY -2.64ppts) 。 
2H26 營運仍 可維持高檔水準 ，持續受惠客戶提升份額 ：展望 手機 PA
因韓系手機持續擴大採用中國供應商，陸系品牌拉貨穩定，且因公司
客戶重心已轉向中高階手機，有效降低記憶體漲價對低階手機市場的
衝擊 。 公司也持續受惠於美系 IDM 廠退出中國市場及中高階手機拉貨
潮，帶動手機 PA 出貨量與 ASP 成長。 Wi-Fi 業務方面， Wi-Fi 7 滲透
率增加帶動 PA 需求，但傳統 Wi-Fi 5/6 部分 市場被矽鍺取代，展望
Router 需求為持平至小幅成長，而手機 Wi-Fi 則因大陸客戶逐漸取代
美系 IDM 廠而成長。 無人機 Wifi PA 因傳輸距離增加及高解析度圖片
回傳需求，客戶持續規格更新及提升需求量，公司現無人機客戶由過
往 1~2 家增加至 4~5 家，成長顯著。  
GaAs 及 InP PD 有望成為第二成長動能， 預期於 2028 年量產 ：公司
在 2026 年 8 月通過 28 億元資本支出 ， 用於擴建無塵室及優化生產線 ，
建立 InP 相關產品製程，預估無塵室於 2027 年底擴建完成。 光通訊業
務方面， 公司 除應用於 Datacom 短距傳輸 VCSE L 外，佈局 GaAs 及
InP PD ，並已與 多位 客戶進行開發與測試 。GaAs PD 進展較為快速，
預估最快將於 2027 年底至 2028 年初進入量產 ，而InP PD 進度較 GaAs 
PD 晚約半年，約於 2028 年年中量產。  
公司 過往 營收有 7-8 成來自手機 PA，在 PA 客戶持續提升份額、無人
機及非射頻相關應用持續顯著成長外，光通訊業務除 VCSEL 外， PD
有望在 2028 年貢獻營收，成為除手機 PA 外下一個主要成長動能。我
們預估 2026 、2027 年 EPS 分別為 5.17 、5.33 元，維持前次報告 本益
比倍數 ，基於 2027 年預估 EPS x 32 倍本益比，目標價為 171 元， 調
升評等至買進 之投資評等建議。 風險因子 ：手機 市場需求不佳 、非射
頻營收不如預期 。

## Document 586: 宏观点评：经济总量边际回升，结构分化延续

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观点评：经济总量边际回升，结构分化延续.pdf
Report source: 五矿证券
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请仔细阅读本报告末页声明                                                                                             Page 1 / 11

证券研究报告 | 宏观点评 
[Table_Main]  
经济总量边际回升，结构分化延续

[Table_Invest] 
宏观点评  
日期： 2026.10.23

[Table_Author]  分析师  尤春野 
登记编码：S0950523100001 
 ： 13817489814 
 ： youchunye1@wkzq.com.cn  
[Table_DocReport]

报告摘要  
海外宏观： 工业仍有支撑， 就业降温与通胀反弹并存。9 月全球制造业 PMI 升
至 53.0，AI 及设备投资支撑工业，但美国新增非农仅 2.9 万人，三个月 均值
约 5.1 万人， 工资增速放缓。 名义消费仍有韧性， 收入和信心走弱则约束 后续
增长。能源涨价带动美国 CPI 同比升至 3.7%，核心 CPI 也回升至 2.6% 。就
业与物价走势的背离加剧美联储政策两难， 高利率仍是基准环境； 能源冲 击向
核心价格的传导决定后续收紧压力，就业降温则提高继续加息的门槛。  
国内宏观：经济总量边际回升，私人需求修复仍慢。 三季度实际 GDP 同比增
长 4.5%，较二季度提高 0.2 个百分点；名义增速升至 6.0%，为 202 2 年 二季
度以来最高， 有助于居民收入和企业营收改善。 工业、 出口和部分服务消 费提
供支撑，9 月工业增加值、 出口分别增长5.5%、36.3%，高技术制造业和 电子
设备制造业增速快于总体。 但居民消费倾向下降、 制造业产能利用率偏低 ，表
明需求恢复仍不充分。9 月社零同比仅增长 1.1%；我们测算固定资产投 资当
月同比下降 7.2%， 降幅收窄， 但房地产销售、资金和建设链条继续承压。PP I
同比上涨 5.0%主要集中于上游， 终端定价仍弱， 社融增量较多依赖政 府债券 ，
居民信用疲弱。四季度增长持续性取决于居民支出和民间资本开支能 否接续 。 
政策：逆周期调节加力，着力打通资金与需求传导。 财政政策强调增量储备、
加快债券使用和提高资金形成实物工作量的效率， 重点观察地方配套与项 目支
付。10 月起首套购房贷款财政贴息覆盖部分中低总价刚需，有利于降低 购房
负担， 效果仍需结合成交、 合格贷款发放及保交付协同验证。 央行重申不 预设
汇率目标、 保持双向弹性， 并防范急剧贬值风险； 贸易顺差和相对低通胀 支撑
人民币中期渐进升值判断。中美有限降税框架 改善外贸预期，“人工 智能 + ”
政策进一步强调应用和商业转化。 
大类资产： 高利率压制风险偏好， 配置兼顾盈利质量与防御价值。近一个月美
元与美债长端利率上行，科技股和贵金属承压； 10 月 5 日至 16 日，10 年期
美债收益率回落 22 个基点，科技股仍下跌，反映市场更加重视 AI 投入 的收
入与现金流回报。 台积电业绩及资本开支仍强， 产业趋势内应重点验证真 实订
单、应用变现和可持续盈利。同期 10 年期实际利率下降 21 个基点，降 低黄
金持有成本，增长与 AI 盈利不确定性提高分散风险的需求。经过回调震 荡，
若实际利率回落延续， 黄金抗风险价值值得重视； 白银工业属性更强，波 动风
险更高。 国内利率债仍受私人信用偏弱支撑， 久期配置需跟踪财政落地与 债券
供给。 
风险提示：  1、中东冲突再度升级； 
2、中国经济超预期下行。

### Page 2

请仔细阅读本报告末页声明                                                                                  Page 2 / 11

[Table_Page]  宏观点评  
2026 年 10 月 23 日

海外宏观：工业仍有支撑，就业降温与通胀反弹并存 
制造业扩张延续，成本压力同步抬升  
全球制造业继续扩张，但增长与价格的组合发生了变化。 9 月全球制造业 PMI 升至 53.0，较
8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 美国ISM 制造业 PMI 为 54.5， 较上月微降0.1 个百分点，新 订单却
由 53.7 升至 55.3，制造业采购需求仍有支撑。欧元区制造业 PMI 由 52.7 升至 52.9 ，日本
由 54.9 降至 54.1， 均处扩张区间。 与此同时， 美国ISM 制造业价格指数由 71.1 升至 7 7.9，
成本压力明显增强。AI 及相关设备投资、供应链采购支撑工业活动 ，但能源和原材料涨价也
在挤压下游利润，制造业景气回升向居民实际需求的传导仍不充分。  
图表 1：全球制造业保持扩张，工业景气仍有支撑

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
就业和收入放缓，消费韧性受到考验  
美国就业市场降温。 10 月 2 日公布的 9 月非农就业仅增加 2.9 万人， 低于修订后的8 月 13.3
万人，7、8 月合计下修 6 万人；按最新数据，三个月平均新增就业约 5.1 万人。失 业率由
4.1%升至 4.2%，劳动参与率由 61.6%升至 61.8%，失业率上行同时受到劳动力供给 恢复影
响。平均时薪同比增速由约 3.1%降至约 3.0%，企业招聘和工资扩张均趋谨慎。当前 就业总
量仍较稳定，但新增收入动力减弱，会逐步提高居民维持消费增长的难度。  
美国消费尚有韧性，收入和信心却提示后续增长承压。 9 月零售和餐饮销售额名义环比增长
0.8%，剔除汽车和汽油后仅增长 0.2%，能源涨价对总额形成了明显支撑。 9 月 30 日 公布的
8 月实际个人消费支出环比增长 0.6%， 实际可支配收入却基本持平， 支出快于收入的 状态需
要储蓄或融资补充。同期公布的二季度实际 GDP 终值环比折年增长 2.2%，较前次上修 0.7
个百分点，说明此前私人需求底盘仍强；但 10 月密歇根消费者信心初值降至 46.3 ，低于 9
月 48.1。 增长的关键已转向就业与实际收入能否接续支撑消费， 而非仅看名义支出的高增 速。 
能源带动通胀回升， 美联储面临更复杂的取舍 
美国通胀重新抬升，工资放缓尚未抵消能源成本冲击。 9 月 CPI 同比由 3.4%升至 3.7%，环
比上涨 0.6%；汽油价格环比上涨 9.1% ，贡献了总体环比涨幅的一半以上。核心 CP I同比也
由 2.4%升至 2.6%，环比上涨 0.3%，显示价格压力已不只体现在总体指标。 10 月消 费者一
年期通胀预期初值由 4.6%升至 4.7% ，而平均工资增速已低于总体物价涨幅，居民实 际购买
力面临挤压。就业增量放缓与核心价格反弹同时出现，美联储需要在抑制二次传导和 避免需
求过度降温之间权衡，能源能否持续影响核心商品和服务将决定后续政策压力。

欧洲景气修复与能源再通胀并存，日本扩张速度有所放慢。 9 月欧元区综合 PMI 由 52.0 升
至 53.1，服务业由 51.6 升至 53.0，德国改善较为明显；欧元区 HICP 同比则由 3.2% 升至
3.8%，能源同比上涨 18.9%，核心通胀由 2.4%升至 2.5%。总体物价的反弹主要来自能 源，
但需求回升也降低了立即宽松的必要性， 欧洲央行10 月公布的会议纪要继续强调逐次 会议、
依数据决策。日本制造业和服务业 PMI 分别由 54.9、52.5 降至 54.1、51.3，仍保持 扩张，
但速度放缓。 
国内宏观：总量小幅回稳，私人部门需求修复仍慢 
三季度增长回升，收入向消费转化不足  
三季度经济增长小幅回升，内需修复的基础仍需巩固。 三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5%，较
二季度提高 0.2 个百分点， 季调环比由0.9%升至 1.2%，前三季度累计增长 4.6%。第 三产业
当季增长 5.2%， 第二产业增长3.4%，后者较二季度 3.0%改善，但仍低于服务业。工业 、出
口与部分服务消费共同支撑总量回稳，固定资产投资和 房地产开发链条则继续收缩。 值得关
注之处在于名义 GDP 同比增长 6%，是 2022 年二季度以来的最高值， 有助于居民收 入和企
业营收状况的改善。总量增速的回升为四季度稳增长创造了较好起点，持续性取决于 居民支
出和私人资本开支能否跟进。

65.3%。工资性收入和经营净收入分别增长 5.3% 、6.6% ，财产净收入仅增长 1.6% ，收入中
位数增速 4.6%也低于均值。 资产回报偏弱、 不同家庭收入恢复不均， 使一部分新增收 入继续
用于储蓄和资产负债表修复。9 月调查失业率由 5.3%降至 5.2% ，但制造业和非制造业 PMI
就业指数仍低于 50，消费的持续回升还需要就业与财富预期改善。  
生产与外需保持韧性，行业分化继续扩大  
景气调查显示生产恢复快于新增需求。 9 月制造业 PMI 由 49.8 升至 50.1，生产指数由 50.4
升至 51.7，新订单却由 50.6 略降至 50.5，生产与订单的差距 扩大至 1.2 个百分点； 新出口
订单为 50.0。 大型企业PMI 为 50.6，中小企业分别为 49.7 和 48.9 ， 扩张尚未均匀覆 盖企业
群体。非制造业商务活动指数由 49.0 升至 50.2，建筑业与服务业均回到荣枯线上方 ，但新
订单只有 45.7 和 46.7。季节性生产与施工修复已经出现，企业新增订单仍弱，这会 限制补
库存、招聘和投资的持续扩张。 
工业增长较快，产能利用率却暴露出行业需求的错位。 9 月规上工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，
较 8 月加快 0.3 个百分点；高技术制造业、电子设备制造业分别增长 15.3%、16.4% ，仍显
著快于总体，但增速较 8 月有所回落。 三季度工业产能利用率为 73.1%， 较二季度仅提 高0.1
个百分点，同比下降 1.5 个百分点；制造业为 73.3%，较二季度下降 0.2 个百分点。 电子设
备制造业升至 80.2%， 汽车制造业却由70.8%降至 66.2%， 非金属矿物制品业仅 61.2% 。新
产业订单与传统工业闲置产能同时存在，工业增加值较快增长，并未带来制造业产能 利用率
同步回升。

外贸继续支撑生产，科技制造的拉动进一步集中。 按美元计价，9 月出口同比增长 36.3%，
高于 8 月 24.9%；进口增长 31.0%，货物贸易顺差约 1335 亿美元。集成电路、自动 数据处
理设备及零部件、电动载人汽车出口金额分别增长约 136%、93%和 102%，与全球 电子及
设备投资需求相呼应。较强出口有助于消化部分工业供给，进口增长则兼有中间品采 购和能
源价格的影响。外需对生产的支撑与国内消费、建筑投资偏弱可以同时持续；后续应 关注海
外真实订单、AI 资本开支和贸易政策落实， 这会影响到 高出口增速能否延续。

商品消费低位回升，投资与房地产仍承压  
商品零售边际改善，汽车和地产链消费仍是主要拖累。 9 月社零同比增长 1.1%，高于 8 月
0.4%，季调环比由下降 0.16%转为增长 0.35%；剔除汽车后的零售增长 3.9%，餐饮 收入增
长 2.1%。限额以上汽车类零售下降 21.2%，降幅继续扩大，家具和建筑装潢材料分 别下降
3.0%、9.2%， 通讯器材却增长31.1%。 耐用品消费仍呈明显分层，部分科技新品需求 较强，
住房相关消费和汽车需求偏弱。前三季度服务零售增长 5.0%，商品零售增长 1.0% ， 服务消
费为总需求提供了缓冲；但居民整体消费倾向下降，决定了消费修复仍需从品类刺激 转向收
入和预期的持续改善。 
投资单月降幅收窄， 设备更新强于建筑安装。我们测算 9 月固定资产投资当月同比下降 7.2%，
较 8 月下降 10.6%收窄；制造业投资当月下降约 0.9%，好于 8 月下降 6.1%。前三季 度固定
资产投资累计仍下降 7.2%，制造业投资下降 2.1% ，房地产开发投资下降 20.6%，民 间投资
下降 10.4%；国家统计局现行口径的基础设施投资下降 4.2%。设备工器具购置增长 9.7% ，
电子设备制造业投资增长 9.6%，建筑安装工程却下降 10.0%。设备采购和部分新产 业资本
开支已有改善， 但更广泛的项目建设和民营投资仍缺乏订单与回报支撑。 财政资金投放之 后，
需进一步跟踪招标、开工和实际支付，判断投资改善能否持续。

2026 年 10 月 23 日

新房成交疲弱压低现金回笼，融资不足又限制建设与交付。住建部门统计的前三季度 二手房
网签面积增长 10.7%，显示存量住房交易仍有需求，却难以直接补充开发商回款。房 地产修
复既要改善购房可负担性，也要让合格项目资金覆盖建设期，销售 企稳才能逐步传至 开工与
投资。 
成本上涨与终端偏弱并存，信用依赖财政托底  
价格回升主要集中在生产链上游，终端定价能力改善较慢。 9 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%，核心
CPI 为 1.0%，服务价格上涨 0.8% ，食品和房租分别下降 1.7%、0.6%；PPI 同比却由 3.8%
升至 5.0%， 采掘、 原材料和加工工业分别上涨25.4%、9.4% 、3.4% ， 生活资料仍下降0.1% 。
能源和资源价格改善上游名义收入，同时抬高中下游成本，消费与住房相关行业的售 价仍受
需求约束。我们测算三季度 GDP 平减指数同比约上涨 1.4%，与二季度约 1.5%接近 ，反映
工业品涨价尚未广泛扩散至全经济价格。后续盈利修复的广度，要看终端订单与售价 能否接
续改善。 
图表 7：通胀水平上升

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
融资总量获得季末与政府债支持，居民信用依旧疲弱。 9 月新增社融 3.55 万亿元，与上年同
月接近； 政府债券净融资1.54 万亿元， 占当月社融约 43%， 同比多增3469 亿元，社 融口径
人民币贷款 1.27 万亿元，同比少增 3347 亿元。金融机构新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿 元，其
中企业中长期贷款 8900 亿元， 住户贷款却几乎没有净增长，上年同月为 3890 亿元。社融存
量同比增速由 7.2%降至 7.1%，M1、M2 分别降至 3.2% 、7.2%。政府融资和企业季 末投放
托住了流量，居民支出与内生扩表需求仍弱；四季度信用改善的关键，是财政资金能 否顺利
进入项目支付、供应商现金流和居民收入，而非债券发行规模本身。  
政策：逆周期调节加力，重点打通资金与需求 
财政与住房政策提供更直接的需求支持  
逆周期政策更加重视增量储备与存量资金使用效率 。9 月 28 日国务院常务会议研究宏观政
策发力提效、促进有效投资，要求“加大宏观政策逆周期调节力度 ”，部署加快债券发行 使用和
重大项目开工，用好地方政府债务结存限额，并研究稳定房地产、促进就业增收的措施。 10
月 9 日财政部发布《2026 年上半年中国财政政策执行情况报告》 ，进一步强调提高政 府债券
资金形成实物工作量的效率、视经济运行谋划增量政策。三季度总量回稳与私人需求 偏弱并
存，政策需要同时兼顾项目建设和居民收入。四季度可重点跟踪结存限额使用、地方 配套资
金和项目支付进度，观察融资支持向实际需求的转化。

### Page 7

请仔细阅读本报告末页声明                                                                                  Page 7 / 11

[Table_Page]  宏观点评  
2026 年 10 月 23 日

行、 金融监管总局发布 《关于实施居民购房贷款贴息政策的通知》 ， 自10 月 1 日起暂行 一年，
对新发放、 用于购买首套且面积不超过120 平方米、总价不超过 150 万元住房的合格 商业贷
款给予贴息，年化 1 个百分点、最长五年，每户可贴息本金上限 100 万元。政策把财 政支持
由收储和项目端延伸至购房家庭，有利于小城市和城镇房地产 市场的刚需释放，对高 房价城
市及改善性需求的直接覆盖相对有限。后续效果要结合 10 月以后合格贷款发放、新 房成交
和库存变化判断，并与开发融资及保交付安排协同。  
汇率与贸易政策稳定外部预期  
央行重申汇率市场化， 同时更加 关注急剧贬值带来的金融风险。 10 月 8 日， 中国人民银行发
布《中国人民银行关于人民币汇率的政策立场 》，明确不预设汇率目标水平 、不干预长期趋 势 ，
保持弹性与双向浮动；对于短期大幅波动 ，则强调防范“尤其是短期急剧贬值 ”影响金融 稳定 。
我们认为，这一立场为货币政策保持自主性提供空间，也表明稳定预期的重点在于避 免贬值
预期自我强化。较高贸易顺差和相对低通胀为人民币中期渐进升值提供支撑，短期利 差、美
元走势与跨境资金偏好仍会带来双向波动，汇率调整更可能缓慢而有反复。  
中美形成有限降税框架， 外贸政策的可预期性有所改善。 9 月 28 日商务部公布第八轮经贸磋
商相关成果，双方拟就各自约 300 亿美元的自对方进口产品对等降税，并将部分关税 和非关
税措施的暂停安排延至 2027 年 1 月 10 日； 降税在双方完成国内程序后同步实施。 贸 易理事
会、农业工作组等机制为后续磋商提供渠道。相对于双边贸易总量，本轮覆盖规模有 限，短
期作用更偏向降低部分商品的不确定性、稳定订单安排。其对出口的实际拉动仍取决 于最终
产品清单、生效时间与海外需求。 
海外高利率约束未解除， AI 政策更加注重应用 
美联储仍将通胀风险置于政策权衡的核心。 10 月 7 日公布的 9 月议息会议纪要显示，多数
与会者在当时判断年内进一步加息一次可能适宜 ；9 月会议已将目标区间提高至 3. 75%—
4.00%。此后公布的就业和 CPI 分别强化了增长放缓与通胀回升的风险，下一次会议 需要在
新数据基础上重新权衡。我们预计高利率维持仍是基准环境，实际就业降温会提高继 续加息
的门槛，核心通胀若持续加速又会限制宽松空间。  
国内产业政策将应用成效作为 AI 投入的重要牵引 。10 月 16 日国务院常务会议听取“人工智
能+”行动进展，要求“以落地应用牵引技术创新 ”，加强算法、算电协同和高质量数据供 给 ，推
进供需对接与中试验证。政策支持由基础设施延伸至场景和商业转 化，有利于技术向 实体经
济扩散。对投资和企业经营而言，后续应更重视付费订单、单位成本下降和生产率改 善，推
动 AI 投入形成可持续现金流。 
大类资产：高利率压制风险偏好 
近一个月由于美国长端利率再度上升，全球风险资产尤其是非美资产受到较大压制。 按 9 月
17 日至 10 月 16 日统一收盘窗口， 标普500 上涨 0.28%， 纳斯达克下跌1.05%； 沪深30 0、
创业板和恒生科技分别下跌 6.49%、15.95%、4.70%，中国长债指数上涨 1.62%。布 伦特原
油上涨 5.63%，南华工业品下跌 3.14%，伦敦现货黄金和白银分别下跌 4.10%、10. 01%，
美元指数上涨 1.55%。能源供给风险支撑油价，国内需求与盈利预期约束权益，贵金 属则经
历美元、利率和仓位调整的共同压力。

### Page 8

请仔细阅读本报告末页声明                                                                                  Page 8 / 11

[Table_Page]  宏观点评  
2026 年 10 月 23 日

图表 8：主要资产价格近一个月涨幅

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
10 月上旬以来的美债利率回落，主要体现实际利率下降。 10 月 5 日至 16 日，10 年期美债
收益率由 5.31%降至 5.09%，30 年期由 5.66%降至 5.43%，10 年期 TIPS 实际收 益率由
2.95%降至 2.74%；名义与实际利率之差所反映的通胀补偿约维持在 2.35%附近。就 业增量
偏低与信心走弱，使增长预期和防御需求对市场的影响增大。  
科技股定价正在更加重视 AI 投入的收入和现金流回报。 10 月 5 日至 16 日，美债 10 年期收
益率下降 22 个基点，同期纳斯达克下跌 4.87%，创业板下跌 11.58%，说明利率回落 尚未扭
转盈利预期和风险偏好的调整。 产业基本面仍有强势环节：台积电 10 月 15 日披露三 季度美
元营收 467.2 亿美元、同比增长 41.2%，并维持全年 600 亿—640 亿美元资本开支计划 。上
游收入和扩产仍强，市场的担忧更多指向资本开支能否持续、终端付费需求能否覆盖 折旧与
运营成本。权益配置需更加关注企业盈利兑现质量，包括收入、订单、利润率和现金 流等指
标，在产业趋势内提高对盈利兑现的要求。 
经过前期回调与震荡，黄金的配置条件正边际改善。 10 月 5 日至 16 日，金价上涨 1.15%，
而白银继续下跌 6.65%；实际利率回落降低了持有黄金的机会成本，增长与 AI 盈利 不确定
性则提高了组合分散风险的需求。世界黄金协会数据显示， 9 月全球黄金 ETF 仍净 流入约
100 亿美元，配置资金未随价格下跌全面撤 离。我们认为，若实际利率回落能够延续 ，黄金
的抗风险价值值得重新重视。白银兼具更强工业属性和价格弹性，在需求预期走弱时 波动更
大，其组合功能与黄金存在区别。美元再次走强或能源冲击推高利率，仍可能扰动贵 金属的
修复节奏。 
配置上应同时重视现金流质量与防御价值。 国内利率债受益于资金价格回落与私人信用偏弱，
可作为组合防御资产， 久期则需结合财政落地和债券供给调整。 权益重点关注AI 产业 链真实
订单、应用变现和可持续盈利，国内政策受益方向以销售、开工和信用改善为验证。 贵金属
配置更重视黄金分散风险的作用，把握震荡中的配置机会；白 银和原油更适合结合工 业周期
与事件变化进行战术判断。

投资评级说明  
投资建议的评级标准 
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为股票评级和
行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告
发布日后 6 到 12 个月内的相对市场表现， 也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 到12个月内的公司股价 （或
行业指数） 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的
涨跌幅作为基准。 其中：A 股市场以沪深300指
数为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准； 美国市
场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普500 指数为基准。 
 评级  说明  
股票
评级 
买入 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报在20%及以上； 
增持 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报介于5%～20%之间； 
持有 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报介于-10%～5%之间； 
卖出 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报在-10%及以下； 
无评级 预期对于个股未来 6 个月市场表现与基准指数相比无明确观点。 
行业
评级 
看好 预期行业整体回报高于基准指数整体水平10%以上； 
中性 预期行业整体回报介于基准指数整体水平-10%～10%之间； 
看淡 预期行业整体回报低于基准指数整体水平-10%以下。

Investment Rating Definitions 
The rating criteria of investment 
recommendations 
The ratings contained herein are classified 
into company ratings and sector ratings 
(unless otherwise stated). The rating 
criteria is the relative market performance 
betw een 6 and 12 months after the report’s 
date of issue, i.e. based on the range of 
rise and fall of the company's stock price 
(or industry index) compared to the 
benchmark index. Specifically, the CSI 300 
Index is the benchmark index of the A-
share market. The Hang Seng Index is the 
benchmark index of the HK market. The 
NASDAQ Composite Index or the S&P 500 
Index is the benchmark index of the U.S. 
market. 
 Ratings Definitions 
Company 
Ratings 
BUY Stock return is expected to outperform the benchmark index by more than 20%; 
ACCUMULATE Stock relative performance is expected to range betw een 5% and 20%; 
HOLD Stock relative performance is expected to range betw een -10% and 5%; 
SELL Stock return is expected to underperform the benchmark index by more than 10%; 
NOT RATED No clear view  of the stock relative performance over the next 6 months. 
Sector 
Ratings

CAUTIOUS Overall sector return is expected to underperform the benchmark index by more than 10%.

### Page 1

EU－中国第２回貿易投資協議（TIC）メカニズムでの合意事項

（資料）EU中国第２回貿易投資協議（TIC）メカニズム共同声明

１. ＥＵの対中国貿易不均衡は2026年も拡大が続いており、対策が急務となっている。 
２. 10月 8～9日のＥＵと中国の第２回貿易投資協議（ＴＩＣ）でのＥＵ側の最大の成果は
ハイブリッド車（ＨＥＶ）のセーフガード（緊急輸入制限）での合意であろう。27年の
中国への数量割当は 26年実績を大きく下回る 35万台と報じられているが、中国は他の
輸入先への割当量より低くならないとの認識の下で受け入れたようだ。 
３. ＥＵは 10月 15～16日の首脳会議でマクロ経済不均衡是正のための「新たな政策手段」
の導入や公共調達・公的支援にＥＵ原産要件を導入する「産業促進法（ＩＡＡ） 」の立法
に向けたプロセスを加速することで合意した。 
４. ＥＵと中国は貿易投資制限と対抗措置の応酬を回避したい思いでは一致しているが、そ
の一方で、ＥＵは対象国を限定しない形ながら、マクロ経済不均衡対策の強化に動き、
中国は、差別的取り扱いを受けることを警戒し、牽制している。ＥＵの新たな動きをき
っかけとするＴＩＣでの合意の撤回や協議の停滞は十分に想定できよう。 
５. ＥＵの貿易不均衡対策は、中国を念頭に置いたものでも、日本企業に供給網の再編を迫
るものとなり得る。動向を注視する必要がある。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-23

（ 対中輸入構造もダイナミックに変化、急務となる対中貿易不均衡拡大策 ） 
ＥＵの中国からの輸入構造もダイナミックに変化している（図表４）。2001年 12月の中国の世
界貿易機関（ＷＴＯ）加盟後の「チャイナ・ショック 1.0」では労働集約的な雑製品が主要な輸入
製品だったが、その割合は、かつての半分程度まで減っている。化学製品は、「チャイナ・ショック
1.0」期にはＥＵの比較優位産業であったが、中国からの輸入は着実に拡大し、21年以降、貿易収
支はＥＵ側の赤字に転じ、赤字幅は拡大している。機械・輸送機器が、ＥＵの中国から輸入で最も
高い割合を占める構造は不変だが、2010年代半ばまでの主力が通信機器（スマホなど） 、事務用・

（ ＥＵはＴＩＣを通じ中国と対話しつつ、協議上の立場強化の観点からも政策手段拡充に動く ） 
今月 8～9日には、ＥＵと中国の第２回貿易投資協議（ＴＩＣ）が中国・北京で開催、ＥＵのシ
ェフチョビッチ欧州委員（貿易担当）と中国の王文濤商務相が協議した。 
ＥＵ側の狙いは、合意に基づく対応策の実行で、無秩序な対抗措置の応酬を回避し、中国からの
輸入急増による欧州の産業空洞化に歯止めを掛けることにあると思われる。ＥＵは対話姿勢を維持
する一方、協議における立場を強化する観点からも、後述のとおり、不均衡に対処するツールの行
使や範囲の拡大を進めつつある。 
中国にとって、輸出市場としてのＥＵは 2025年には米国を上回り、重要性を増している。中国
国内の需要低迷で輸出傾斜を強めていることもあり、ＴＩＣの枠組みを、ＥＵ側の要望に不利益に
ならない範囲で応え、ＥＵの保護主義傾斜を牽制する機会として活用する意図があると思われる。

（ 第２回ＴＩＣのＥＵにとっての最大の成果はＨＥＶでの合意 ） 
第２回ＴＩＣでは、成果として、6月 29日に実施された第 1回協議で作業部会を立ち上げた①
貿易・投資の均衡化、②輸出管理、③知的財産権、④ＷＴＯ改革の4領域、16項目の合意が公表さ
れた（表紙図表参照） 。 
16項目のうち、最も多い10項目が①の貿易・投資の均衡化に関するものである。ＥＵ側の最大
の成果は、中国からの輸入が急増するハイブリッド車（ＨＥＶ）に関する合意（第３項）である。

### Page 5

図表 11

図表 12

（ ＨＥＶでの合意の他の主要輸入相手国への影響は内容次第 ） 
今回の合意は、こうした中国製ＨＥＶの輸入急増に歯止めを掛け、ＥＵ市場における熾烈な価格
競争の圧力を緩和することにつながると見られる。但し、日本を始めとする中国以外のＨＥＶの輸
入先への影響は、セーフガードの全容を踏まえて判断する必要があろう。 
ＥＵと中国のＴＩＣの合意文書のＨＥＶ関連の項目は「ＷＴＯルールに適合する枠組み」とある
のみで、詳細の公式発表はない。報道によれば「４年間にわたるセーフガード（緊急輸入制限）、27
年の中国への数量割当は 35万台、以後は毎年 6％拡大」とされる。当初の割当が 35万台であれば、
25年実績の 30.2万台を上回るが、26年１～８月期の実績を大きく下回る。それでも中国が合意し
たのは、無差別を原則とするＷＴＯルールに適合するならば、24年以降の急伸で築いた優位を維
持できるとの判断があるようだ。中国商務部の報道官は、10月 12日の会見で「中国に付与される
割当量は EUへの他の輸入先に対する割当量よりも低くならない」との認識を示している3。 
ＥＵのＨＥＶの主な輸入先である日本、韓国、英国はいずれも EUと自由貿易協定を締結して
おり、トルコは関税同盟を形成しているが、ＨＥＶのセーフガードでどのような割当量が設定され
るのかは現時点では不透明だ。先行例となるＥＵが 18年 7月に発動した鉄鋼セーフガード（輸入
数量に関税割当を設け、枠を超えた輸入に25％の追加関税を賦課）では、ＥＵとの単一市場を形成
するＥＥＡ（欧州経済領域）参加国のノルウェー、アイスランド、リヒテンシュタインは対象外と
されたが、日本、トルコ、韓国は適用対象となった（英国は当時ＥＵ加盟国） 。鉄鋼セーフガードに
替わり 26年 7月 1日から施行された鉄鋼輸入規制（無税輸入枠を 24年基準比 47％削減、超過分
には 50％関税を適用）でもＥＥＡは除外、ＦＴＡ・ＥＰＡ締約国は無税枠の割当の優遇はあるが、
割当超過分への 50％関税は適用される。

3 MOFCOM Spokesperson’s Remarks on the China-EU Understandings Reached in Consultations on Hybrid 
Vehicles and BEVs, October 16, 2026

### Page 6

（ 中国はＥＵの貿易投資障壁の引き上げの動きを牽制 ） 
貿易・投資均衡化の10項目のうち、主に中国側の関心事項であったのは、ＢＥＶの「価格約束」
の実施と審査（第４項） 、 「外国補助金規則（ＦＳＲ） 」に関わる対話（第８項） 、 「クリーンエネルギ
ー事業へのＥＵの資金の利用に関するガイダンス」 （第９項）である。 
「価格約束」は、ＥＵのアンチ補助金規則に基づくＢＥＶメーカーへの相殺関税の免除する中国
側の要望を反映した措置である（図表 13）。26年 1月 12日に申請ガイダンスが公表されたが、適
用の条件が厳しいこともあり、これまでの適用事例は１例、それも独ＶＷ（安徽）の子会社に留ま
る（図表 14） 。この制度の適用のさらなる拡大を求めていると思われる。

図表 13

図表 14 独仏共同提案の主な内容

（資料）French-German non-paper “Strengthening 
European resilience, addressing global 
macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting 
practices, 5 October 2026

ＦＳＲは、ＥＵが外国政府からの補助金による競争の歪みを是正するために、外国政府の補助金
を受けた企業による企業買収やＥＵの公共調達入札などを審査し、是正措置を認める規則である。
中国を特定した規則ではなく、ＵＡＥの企業も審査・是正措置の対象となっているが、中国の適用
例が多く、2024年の中国中車（CRRC）ブルガリアの鉄道車両調達のように入札撤退に追い込まれ
概要
〇　個別の輸出企業・製品が、最低輸入価格（ＭＩＰ）、年間
　　販売数量、ＥＵ域内での販売価格、販売チャンネル、ＢＥ
　　Ｖ関連の産業への投資計画等などを盛り込んだＵＴを
　　提出
〇　欧州委がＵＴが「補助金の有害な影響を排除するため
　　に関税と同等の効果を持つ」などの要件への適合性を
　　判断、対象企業・製品の相殺関税を免除
〇　ＵＴの条件への違反があれば相殺関税は復活
適用例 VW（安徽） CUPRA Tavascan model　（26年2月）
（資料）欧州委員会実施規則
EUの中国製BEVの「価格約束（ＵＴ）」制度

### Page 7

（ ＥＵ首脳はＴＩＣ後の会合で不均衡対策の「新たな政策手段」の加速などで合意 ） 
10月 15～16日のＥＵ首脳会議4では、世界的なマクロ経済不均衡対策のため、欧州委員会に「成
果指向の対話」と「既存のツールの効果的な活用」 、さらに緊急で「新たな政策手段」を導入し、政
策手段を拡充する方向性を確認した。 
「新たな政策手段」に関しては、10月 5日に、独仏が世界的なマクロ経済不均衡と市場歪曲的
慣行に対処するための対策をまとめた「非公式提案文書（ノンペーパー） 」で示したＥＵの単一市
場へのアクセス制限（いわゆる「キル・スイッチ」 ）が選択肢となる。独仏の提案では、キル・スイ
ッチについて、特定多数の反対がなければ発動可能とすること、対象国を限定しないこと、あらゆ
る種類のシステミックかつ持続的な市場の歪みに対処できるものとすることなど を提言している
（図表 15）。独仏のノンペーパーでは、市場歪曲的慣行として、 「ダンピング、過剰かつ広範な補助
金、その他の不公正な産業政策措置に加え、通貨の交換可能性に対する制限や為替操作など」を挙
げている。為替レートの問題と世界的な不均衡の相互作用についての共通認識の構築やユーロ圏財
務相会合、欧州中央銀行（ECB）、欧州委員会などのＥＵ機関が、為替問題について関係当局との
対話を行うことについても提言している。

（ 産業促進法（ＩＡＡ）のプロセスも加速、ＥＶ優遇は「欧州製」限定へ ） 
10月の首脳会議では、欧州委員会が 26年 3月に提案した公共調達・公的支援にＥＵ原産要件
を導入する「産業促進法（ＩＡＡ） 」 （図表15）の立法に向けたプロセスを加速する方針でも合意し
た。 
ＩＡＡの焦点の１つはＥＶ優遇策の原産地規制であった。ＩＡＡの原案では、日本などＦＴＡパ
ートナー国等を条件付きで「EU原産」と同等に扱うが、ＥＶ優遇策は「27カ国内での組み立て」
と「金額ベースで車両部品の70％以上の原産割合」を求めた。この規定を巡って、フランスが厳格
な欧州製を主張、ドイツがパートナー国を含むことを主張し対立してきたが、16日に「欧州自動車
産業の将来に関する独仏政府の合意」5を発表した。 ドイツが主張した二酸化炭素排出規制 （ＣＯ２）
の緩和を見返りに、ＥＶ優遇についてはフランスの主張に寄り、一定条件下では英国を含み、ＥＥ
Ａに参加するノルウェーやアイスランドも含まれるが、日本や韓国などＥＵのＦＴＡパートナー国
は対象外となる方向とされる6。

4 European Council meeting (15 and 16 October 2026) – Conclusions 
5 Joint Franco-German Press Statement on the Future of the European Automotive Industry, 16 October 2026  
6 「独仏、EV優遇「欧州製に限定」日本 EUに修正要求へ」2026年 10月 17日日経電子版

### Page 8

図表 15 欧州委員会が 26年３月に提案した「産業促進法（IAA）」の概要

（＊１）製品・支援制度に応じて EU原産要件または低炭素要件を設定 
「公共調達・公的支援」では、EUと FTA・関税同盟を締結する国や WTO政府調達協定（GPA）加盟国の製品も、
EUの国際約束が適用される場合は「EU原産」として扱う 
但し、「ＥＶの社用車導入支援」、「小型ＥＶへの優遇措置」は、FTA締結国産をＥＵ原産と同等に扱う規定がない 
自動車分野のＥＵ原産要件は「ＥＵ27カ国内での組み立て」と「金額ベースで車両部品の 70％以上の原産割合」 
（＊２）戦略分野の１億ユーロ超の投資が対象

（ ＥＵの貿易不均衡対策は日本企業にも供給網再編を迫るものとなり得る ） 
ＥＵと中国は、27年も１月に閣僚級のオンライン会議を開催、3月に対面での第３回ＴＩＣを
開催することを約束している。定期的に対話の機会を持ち、貿易・投資制限とその対抗措置の拡大
を回避したいとの思いは一致しているようだ。 
その一方で、ＥＵは対象国を限定しない形ながら、マクロ経済不均衡対策の強化に動き、中国は、
新たな政策で差別的取り扱いを受けることを警戒し、牽制している。ＥＵの新たな動きをきっかけ
とするＴＩＣでの合意の撤回や協議の停滞は十分に想定できよう。 
ＥＵの貿易不均衡対策は、中国を念頭においたものでも、日本企業に供給網の再編を迫るものと
なり得る。動向を注視する必要がある。
